People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this. 

I feel a little silly, to be honest, but... well, what made me come in is that my eating has just gotten a little all turned around lately. I'm sure it's nothing terrible, but some days I just completely forget to have lunch. And then other days, I'm a bit embarrassed to admit, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore. 

Is that... does that sound like a good enough reason to come see you? I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing by coming in.

Well, like I mentioned, nothing really has any flavor anymore, so... no, I suppose the enjoyment is mostly gone. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. 

But, um... if I'm being perfectly honest—and I feel a little guilty admitting this—it isn't just the food. I'm a bit embarrassed to say it, but I've noticed I'm like that with... well, with everything lately. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and my book club. I've always loved keeping busy with the girls! But now... I'll get all ready, and then I just leave my bag by the door. I just can't seem to make myself care enough to actually go. 

I feel terrible letting everyone down, and I promise I'm trying my best to push myself. Is it... does it make sense to lose your spark for things like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture.

myself, but I do find myself thinking that if I didn’t wake up one morning, it might be a relief to everyone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I am in that low, colorless state, I am also most caught up in the idea that the family papers and hospital notes Oh, well... let me think. I don't want to give you the wrong timeline and mess up your notes. 

I suppose... well, it's been a little while now. Maybe about a year and two months? Give or take a little bit. It's just been this long, low, colorless stretch for about that long. I hope it's alright that I waited so long to come in... I probably should have said something sooner, shouldn't I? I just didn't want to make a fuss if it was just a silly phase. Did I do the right thing by finally coming in?

Oh, dear... I really hate to admit it, because I know I have so much to be thankful for, and I try so hard not to be a complainer. I always want to be a positive person for everyone, you know?

Well, I know I brought this up a little bit earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much and wasting your time... but yes, I'm afraid so. 

Like I mentioned before with my watercolor class and the quilting circle and my book club... I used to just love being around the girls. I always looked forward to it. I feel so guilty about it, because I love my friends and my family, I truly do! I just don't want you to think I'm antisocial or a bad friend. It's just so hard to be good company right now. Does that answer your question alright? I hope I'm making sense.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I suppose I have been a little slower lately. I really don't want to make a fuss about it, because I know at my age, you can't expect to be sprinting around like a teenager, right? 

But, yes, sometimes my body just feels... weighed down. It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. 

Though, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. It's a bit of a mix, really.

Oh, goodness... well, I know I just said a little bit ago that I felt guilty about missing my get-togethers with the girls, and I hope I'm not contradicting myself now... but overall? No, I don't think I feel worthless.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? I certainly don't want to alarm you, and I promise I don't have any sort of plan to... to hurt myself or anything terrible like that. I know how awful that would be for my family, and I would never want to cause them that kind of pain.

But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be honest with you—there are times when I just think... well, if I went to sleep and just didn't wake up the next morning, it might just be easier. Like it might be a relief, you know? Not just for me, but for everyone else, too. I wouldn't be such a burden or a worry to them anymore. 

I feel so dreadful even saying that out loud. It sounds so ungrateful when I have a good family. Please don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. Is it somewhat normal for someone my age to think that way every now and then? I just want to make sure I'm not making things sound worse than they are.

care enough to go.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: My body feels slowed down lately, like it takes me twice as long to cross the room or button my sweater, though sometimes I pace around the house with the same papers in my hand.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I sit there staring at simple things, like whether to call the pharmacy or open the mail first, and my mind gets so foggy that I end up doing neither.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I do find myself thinking that if I didn’t wake up one morning, it might be a relief to everyone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I am in that low, colorless state, I am also most caught up in the idea that the family papers and hospital notes are carrying hidden messages for me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the sadness and loss of interest have taken up most of this whole period since my sister became ill, even when the record worries were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of several weeks when my mood had eased enough that I was eating and getting dressed more normally, but I still felt sure the dates and names in the records were being changed to signal something.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have stopped teaching my craft classes and have missed calls, appointments, and family tasks I used to handle carefully. I still manage to stay in my home, but I rely more on reminders and put off things that used to be routine.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This whole change has been going on for about a year and five months, since my sister’s health took that serious turn.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The ideas about hidden messages in the family and medical papers started about a year and a month ago.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little slower lately. I really don't want to make a fuss about it, because I know at my age, you can't expect to be sprinting around like a teenager, right? \n\nBut, yes, sometimes my body just feels... weighed down. It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. \n\nThough, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. But I try very hard to focus on the good things and count my blessings. I've always tried to be a good person and do my best for my family, so I don't have those terrible feelings of being completely worthless, no. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound arrogant or prideful for me to say that! I just try not to complain when I have so much to be thankful for. Is that alright? It sounds so ungrateful when I have a good family. Please don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. Is it somewhat normal for someone my age to think that way every now and then? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I know I mentioned earlier that my body can feel a bit heavy or slow sometimes, but as for actual fatigue or losing my energy completely? No, I don't think so. 

I still make sure to get up and do what needs doing around the house every day. I mean, I might need to sit down with a cup of tea in the afternoon to rest my feet, but I think that's just part of being seventy-four, don't you? I try very hard not to just sit around all day, I really do. I like to keep the house tidy and stay on top of things as best I can.

I hope I'm explaining the difference right... I'd hate to confuse you after what I said before about moving slowly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you clear answers. Does that make sense, how I'm describing it?

I mean, maybe some nights I toss and turn a little bit, or maybe I take a little nap in the afternoon sometimes, but nothing that seems too troublesome. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very helpful answer. I hope I'm not missing something important that I should be telling you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. \n\nThough, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I know I just said a little bit ago that I felt guilty about missing my get-togethers with the girls, and I hope I'm not contradicting myself now... but overall? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I really wish I could give you a better answer for that. I know how important it is to stay active and keep busy at my age, I really do. 

I used to just love my little outings. I had a watercolor class, my quilting circle, and a lovely book club that I always looked forward to so much. But lately... well, I feel terrible admitting this, because I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy. But lately, I'll get everything ready, and then... I just leave my bag sitting right there by the door. I just can't seem to make myself care enough to actually go. 

I feel so dreadful saying that out loud. It sounds awful, doesn't it? Like I'm just giving up. I promise you, I'm usually a very involved person, and I hate letting the other ladies down. I hope you don't think poorly of me for skipping them... do you think it's normal to lose interest like that when you're feeling a bit under the weather? I just want to make sure I'm not doing something terribly wrong.

I mean, maybe some nights I toss and turn a little bit, or maybe I take a little nap in the afternoon sometimes, but nothing that seems too troublesome. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very helpful answer. Is it okay that my sleep is mostly just... normal? I hope I'm not missing something important that I should be telling you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little slower lately. I really don't want to make a fuss about it, because I know at my age, you can't expect to be sprinting around like a teenager, right? \n\nBut, yes, sometimes my body just feels... weighed down. It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. \n\nThough, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. It's a bit of a mix, really. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange or contradictory. Is it normal to have both of those things happen? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I know I just said a little bit ago that I felt guilty about missing my get-togethers with the girls, and I hope I'm not contradicting myself now... but overall? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture and answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? No, I don't think I feel worthless. \n\nI mean, I think everyone looks back and wishes they'd done a few things differently here and there, especially at my age. But I try very hard to focus on the good things and count my blessings. I've always tried to be a good person and do my best for my family, so I don't have those terrible feelings of being completely worthless, no. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound arrogant or prideful for me to say that! I just try not to complain when I have so much to be thankful for. Is that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture and answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? I certainly don't want to alarm you, and I promise I don't have any sort of plan to... to hurt myself or anything terrible like that. I know how awful that would be for my family, and I would never want to cause them that kind of pain.\n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be honest with you—there are times when I just think... well, if I went to sleep and just didn't wake up the next morning, it might just be easier. It sounds so ungrateful when I have a good family. Please don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. Is it somewhat normal for someone my age to think that way every now and then? I just want to make sure I'm not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of energy or fatigue most days?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... No, I don't think so. \n\nI still make sure to get up and do what needs doing around the house every day. I mean, I might need to sit down with a cup of tea in the afternoon to rest my feet, but I think that's just part of being seventy-four, don't you? I try very hard not to just sit around all day, I really do. I like to keep the house tidy and stay on top of things as best I can.\n\nI hope I'm explaining the difference right... I'd hate to confuse you after what I said before about moving slowly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you clear answers. Oh... oh my. That's a very difficult question. I... I want to be perfectly honest with you, but I don't want to alarm you or make you think I'm a danger to myself. I promise I'm not. I really don't have any sort of plan to do anything foolish or hurt myself, I assure you of that. 

But... well, since you ask... sometimes, when things feel so very heavy and I feel like such a burden to my family... I do catch myself thinking that... maybe if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up the next morning, it might just be easier for everyone. A bit of a relief, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I haven't really been checking the scale, I'm afraid. But... well, actually, my eating has gotten all turned around lately. That's actually one of the main things that made me realize I should probably come in and see you. 

I feel a bit silly admitting this, because I know at my age I should be much better about my nutrition, and I don't want you to think I'm just letting myself go. But some days... I just completely forget to have lunch. It just slips my mind entirely. And then other days... oh, it's terrible, I'll just stand right there at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets. And the funny thing is, nothing even tastes right to me anymore. 

I know I shouldn't be eating like that, I really do know better. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for not keeping up with my proper meals. Is it... is it normal for your appetite to get all mixed up like that when you're feeling a bit down? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this. \n\nI feel a little silly, to be honest, but... well, what made me come in is that my eating has just gotten a little all turned around lately. I'm sure it's nothing terrible, but some days I just completely forget to have lunch. And then other days, I'm a bit embarrassed to admit, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore. \n\nIs that... does that sound like a good enough reason to come see you? I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing by coming in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I haven't actually stepped on a scale recently... I hope that's alright. I probably should have checked before coming in, shouldn't I? I'm sorry about that. \n\nBut like I was just saying with my eating being so all over the place... I imagine my weight might be fluctuating a little bit. When you forget to eat half the day and then only have those silly sweets later because nothing has any flavor... well, it's bound to do something to your waistline, I suppose. I can certainly start weighing myself every morning for you, if you'd like me to? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy eating or has your interest in food changed recently?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, nothing really has any flavor anymore, so... no, I suppose the enjoyment is mostly gone. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. \n\nBut, um... if I'm being perfectly honest—and I feel a little guilty admitting this—it isn't just the food. I'm a bit embarrassed to say it, but I've noticed I'm like that with... well, with everything lately. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and my book club. I've always loved keeping busy with the girls! But now... I'll get all ready, and then I just leave my bag by the door. I just can't seem to make myself care enough to actually go. \n\nI feel terrible letting everyone down, and I promise I'm trying my best to push myself. Is it... does it make sense to lose your spark for things like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost your spark for things like your watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... let me think. I don't want to give you the wrong timeline and mess up your notes. \n\nI suppose... well, it's been a little while now. Maybe about a year and two months? Give or take a little bit. It's just been this long, low, colorless stretch for about that long. I kept thinking I would just snap out of it, you know? I didn't want to be a bother to anyone, so I just kept trying to put on a brave face and hope the spark would come back on its own. \n\nI hope it's alright that I waited so long to come in... I probably should have said something sooner, shouldn't I? I just didn't want to make a fuss if it was just a silly phase. Did I do the right thing by finally coming in?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I really hate to admit it, because I know I have so much to be thankful for, and I try so hard not to be a complainer. I always want to be a positive person for everyone, you know? \n\nBut... well, yes, I suppose I do. Most mornings, I wake up with this... this very heavy, low feeling. It's almost like all the color has just gone out of the day before I even manage to get dressed. I feel terrible saying that out loud, like I'm just being ungrateful or difficult. I try to brush it off and put on a happy face, I really do. \n\nIs that the sort of thing you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the right way for your notes.\n\nDoctor: Have you also lost interest in being around friends and family?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I brought this up a little bit earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much and wasting your time... but yes, I'm afraid so. \n\nLike I mentioned before with my watercolor class and the quilting circle and my book club... I used to just love being around the girls. I always looked forward to it. But lately, I'll get everything together, and then I just... I leave my bag sitting right by the front door. I just can't seem to muster up the energy or the... the care to actually go and be around everyone. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I love my friends and my family, I truly do! I just don't want you to think I'm antisocial or a bad friend. It's just so hard to be good company right now. Does that answer your question alright? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sleeping or are you sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep... well, I really don't want to make it sound like a big problem, because I know a lot of people my age don't sleep perfectly. It's just a normal part of getting older, isn't it? \n\nI suppose my sleep has been... pretty alright, all things considered. I haven't noticed anything too out of the ordinary, really. I mean, maybe some nights I toss and turn a little bit, or maybe I take a little nap in the afternoon sometimes, but nothing that seems too troublesome. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very helpful answer. Is it okay that my sleep is mostly just... normal? I hope I'm not missing something important that I should be telling you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little slower lately. I really don't want to make a fuss about it, because I know at my age, you can't expect to be sprinting around like a teenager, right? \n\nBut, yes, sometimes my body just feels... weighed down. It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. \n\nThough, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. It's a bit of a mix, really. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange or contradictory. Is it normal to have both of those things happen? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I know I just said a little bit ago that I felt guilty about missing my get-togethers with the girls, and I hope I'm not contradicting myself now... but overall? No, I don't think I feel worthless. \n\nI mean, I think everyone looks back and wishes they'd done a few things differently here and there, especially at my age. But I try very hard to focus on the good things and count my blessings. I've always tried to be a good person and do my best for my family, so I don't have those terrible feelings of being completely worthless, no. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound arrogant or prideful for me to say that! I just try not to complain when I have so much to be thankful for. Is that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture and answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? I certainly don't want to alarm you, and I promise I don't have any sort of plan to... to hurt myself or anything terrible like that. I know how awful that would be for my family, and I would never want to cause them that kind of pain.\n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be honest with you—there are times when I just think... well, if I went to sleep and just didn't wake up the next morning, it might just be easier. Like it might be a relief, you know? Not just for me, but for everyone else, too. I wouldn't be such a burden or a worry to them anymore. \n\nI feel so dreadful even saying that out loud. It sounds so ungrateful when I have a good family. Please don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. Is it somewhat normal for someone my age to think that way every now and then? I just want to make sure I'm not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of energy or fatigue most days?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know I mentioned earlier that my body can feel a bit heavy or slow sometimes, but as for actual fatigue or losing my energy completely? No, I don't think so. \n\nI still make sure to get up and do what needs doing around the house every day. I mean, I might need to sit down with a cup of tea in the afternoon to rest my feet, but I think that's just part of being seventy-four, don't you? I try very hard not to just sit around all day, I really do. I like to keep the house tidy and stay on top of things as best I can.\n\nI hope I'm explaining the difference right... I'd hate to confuse you after what I said before about moving slowly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you clear answers. Does that make sense, how I'm describing it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low and disconnected from things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, let me think... I want to make sure I give you the right timeline. I suppose... well, this sort of low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months now. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound awful, letting it go on for so long without just snapping out of it. Oh... well, I wouldn't say I'm exactly restless, no. I'm usually quite good at sitting quietly. But... well, I do hope this doesn't sound strange, and I don't want you to think I'm losing my wits... but I have noticed I'm moving a bit differently lately. 

Most of the time, I actually feel terribly slowed down. Like... like I'm moving through molasses, if that makes sense? It takes me twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do up the buttons on my cardigan. I feel awful being so sluggish. 

But then... and this is the part I feel a bit foolish mentioning... sometimes I do find myself just pacing around the house. I'll just walk from room to room, carrying the same stack of mail or the newspaper, not really doing anything with it. I know it must look so silly if anyone were watching. I try very hard to stop when I notice I'm doing it. 

Do you think... is it alright that I'm experiencing both of those things? The slowing down and the pacing? I hope I'm explaining it properly so you can understand what I mean.

It's just a normal part of getting older, isn't it? \n\nI suppose my sleep has been... pretty alright, all things considered. I haven't noticed anything too out of the ordinary, really. I mean, maybe some nights I toss and turn a little bit, or maybe I take a little nap in the afternoon sometimes, but nothing that seems too troublesome. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very helpful answer. Is it okay that my sleep is mostly just... normal? I hope I'm not missing something important that I should be telling you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little slower lately. I really don't want to make a fuss about it, because I know at my age, you can't expect to be sprinting around like a teenager, right? \n\nBut, yes, sometimes my body just feels... weighed down. It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. \n\nThough, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. It's a bit of a mix, really. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange or contradictory. Is it normal to have both of those things happen? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I know I just said a little bit ago that I felt guilty about missing my get-togethers with the girls, and I hope I'm not contradicting myself now... but overall? No, I don't think I feel worthless. \n\nI mean, I think everyone looks back and wishes they'd done a few things differently here and there, especially at my age. But I try very hard to focus on the good things and count my blessings. I've always tried to be a good person and do my best for my family, so I don't have those terrible feelings of being completely worthless, no. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound arrogant or prideful for me to say that! I just try not to complain when I have so much to be thankful for. Is that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture and answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? I certainly don't want to alarm you, and I promise I don't have any sort of plan to... to hurt myself or anything terrible like that. I know how awful that would be for my family, and I would never want to cause them that kind of pain.\n\nBut... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... well, I wouldn't say I have a complete lack of reaction to things. I do try very hard to stay engaged, I promise I do. I don't want to seem like a stick-in-the-mud or unappreciative of all the lovely activities available to me. 

But... well, I suppose things have changed a little bit recently. I feel a bit ashamed to admit this, because I know how important it is to keep busy at my age, and I don't want you to think I'm just giving up. But I used to so look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and my book club... they were the highlights of my week. 

Lately, though... oh, it's dreadful of me... I'll get my bag all packed and ready by the door, and then I just... I just can't seem to make myself care enough to actually go. I just leave the bag sitting there. I feel terrible about it, especially when the other ladies are expecting me. I know I should go, and I always tell myself I will next time. 

Is it... is it alright that I'm feeling that way? I do hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or antisocial. I really am trying my best.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... well, I wouldn't say I'm entirely depressed all day long. I really do try to keep a positive outlook, I promise. I know nobody likes a complainer, and I have so much to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself.

But... well, if I'm being completely honest... most mornings I do wake up with this rather heavy, low feeling. It's almost like... like all the color has just washed right out of the day before I've even had a chance to get dressed. It's a bit silly, isn't it? Letting myself feel that way before the day has even begun. 

I do try to shake it off, I really do. I put the kettle on and try to think of pleasant things. I hope you don't think I'm just giving in to it. Is it... is it fairly common to feel that sort of heaviness first thing in the morning? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right sort of information.

I used to just love being around the girls. I always looked forward to it. But lately, I'll get everything together, and then I just... I leave my bag sitting right by the front door. I just can't seem to muster up the energy or the... the care to actually go and be around everyone. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I love my friends and my family, I truly do! I just don't want you to think I'm antisocial or a bad friend. It's just so hard to be good company right now. Does that answer your question alright? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sleeping or are you sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep... well, I really don't want to make it sound like a big problem, because I know a lot of people my age don't sleep perfectly. It's just a normal part of getting older, isn't it? \n\nI suppose my sleep has been... pretty alright, all things considered. I haven't noticed anything too out of the ordinary, really. I mean, maybe some nights I toss and turn a little bit, or maybe I take a little nap in the afternoon sometimes, but nothing that seems too troublesome. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very helpful answer. Is it okay that my sleep is mostly just... normal? I hope I'm not missing something important that I should be telling you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little slower lately. I really don't want to make a fuss about it, because I know at my age, you can't expect to be sprinting around like a teenager, right? \n\nBut, yes, sometimes my body just feels... weighed down. It takes me what feels like twice as long just to walk across the living room or even do simple things like buttoning up my cardigan. I feel so silly admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough. \n\nThough, on the other hand, sometimes I find myself just pacing back and forth in the kitchen, holding the same mail or papers, and I can't seem to settle down. It's a bit of a mix, really. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange or contradictory. Is it normal to have both of those things happen? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I know I just said a little bit ago that I felt guilty about missing my get-togethers with the girls, and I hope I'm not contradicting myself now... but overall? No, I don't think I feel worthless. \n\nI mean, I think everyone looks back and wishes they'd done a few things differently here and there, especially at my age. But I try very hard to focus on the good things and count my blessings. I've always tried to be a good person and do my best for my family, so I don't have those terrible feelings of being completely worthless, no. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound arrogant or prideful for me to say that! I just try not to complain when I have so much to be thankful for. Is that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture and answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? I certainly don't want to alarm you, and I promise I don't have any sort of plan to... to hurt myself or anything terrible like that. I know how awful that would be for my family, and I would never want to cause them that kind of pain.\n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be honest with you—there are times when I just think... well, if I went to sleep and just didn't wake up the next morning, it might just be easier. Like it might be a relief, you know? Not just for me, but for everyone else, too. I wouldn't be such a burden or a worry to them anymore. \n\nI feel so dreadful even saying that out loud. It sounds so ungrateful when I have a good family. Please don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. Is it somewhat normal for someone my age to think that way every now and then? I just want to make sure I'm not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of energy or fatigue most days?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know I mentioned earlier that my body can feel a bit heavy or slow sometimes, but as for actual fatigue or losing my energy completely? No, I don't think so. \n\nI still make sure to get up and do what needs doing around the house every day. I mean, I might need to sit down with a cup of tea in the afternoon to rest my feet, but I think that's just part of being seventy-four, don't you? I try very hard not to just sit around all day, I really do. I like to keep the house tidy and stay on top of things as best I can.\n\nI hope I'm explaining the difference right... I'd hate to confuse you after what I said before about moving slowly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you clear answers. Does that make sense, how I'm describing it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low and disconnected from things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, let me think... I want to make sure I give you the right timeline. I suppose... well, this sort of low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months now. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound awful, letting it go on for so long without just snapping out of it. I've tried very hard to keep a brave face and not make a fuss, I truly have. But yes, I'd say about fourteen months, give or take. \n\nI hope it's alright that I didn't come to see you sooner... I just didn't want to be a bother to anyone or make a mountain out of a molehill, you know? Is that the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to be sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this low for most of the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, yes, I suppose I have. I really hate to admit it, because I know I should be more cheerful, especially when I have so much to be thankful for. But... if I'm being perfectly honest with you, most mornings I do wake up with this very heavy, low feeling. \n\nIt's almost like... like the color has just gone out of the day before I even have a chance to get dressed. I had a watercolor class, my quilting circle, and a lovely book club that I always looked forward to so much. But lately... well, I feel terrible admitting this, because I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy. But lately, I'll get everything ready, and then... You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, my family and friends are so lovely, and they're always checking in on me. I'm so very grateful for them, I really am. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining about them, because they do so much.

I try very hard to be helpful, you know, to not be any trouble. I always tell them they don't need to fuss over me. I can manage perfectly well on my own most days. I certainly wouldn't want anyone to think I was becoming a nuisance or... or dragging them down. 

I suppose... well, sometimes I might worry just a tiny bit that they have their own busy lives and I shouldn't take up too much of their time. But I don't feel like a burden, no. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm being clear that my family is wonderful and I try my best to be a good mother and friend.

I just try not to complain when I have so much to be thankful for. Is that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture and answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very heavy question, isn't it? I certainly don't want to alarm you, and I promise I don't have any sort of plan to... to hurt myself or anything terrible like that. I know how awful that would be for my family, and I would never want to cause them that kind of pain.\n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be honest with you—there are times when I just think... well, if I went to sleep and just didn't wake up the next morning, it might just be easier. Like it might be a relief, you know? Not just for me, but for everyone else, too. I wouldn't be such a burden or a worry to them anymore. \n\nI feel so dreadful even saying that out loud. It sounds so ungrateful when I have a good family. Please don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. Is it somewhat normal for someone my age to think that way every now and then? I just want to make sure I'm not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of energy or fatigue most days?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... No, I don't think so. \n\nI still make sure to get up and do what needs doing around the house every day. I mean, I might need to sit down with a cup of tea in the afternoon to rest my feet, but I think that's just part of being seventy-four, don't you? I try very hard not to just sit around all day, I really do. I like to keep the house tidy and stay on top of things as best I can.\n\nI hope I'm explaining the difference right... I'd hate to confuse you after what I said before about moving slowly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you clear answers. Does that make sense, how I'm describing it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low and disconnected from things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, let me think... I want to make sure I give you the right timeline. I suppose... well, this sort of low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months now. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound awful, letting it go on for so long without just snapping out of it. I've tried very hard to keep a brave face and not make a fuss, I truly have. But yes, I'd say about fourteen months, give or take. \n\nI hope it's alright that I didn't come to see you sooner... I just didn't want to be a bother to anyone or make a mountain out of a molehill, you know? Is that the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to be sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this low for most of the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, yes, I suppose I have. I really hate to admit it, because I know I should be more cheerful, especially when I have so much to be thankful for. But... if I'm being perfectly honest with you, most mornings I do wake up with this very heavy, low feeling. \n\nIt's almost like... like the color has just gone out of the day before I even have a chance to get dressed. I try so hard to push it away, I really do. I don't want to be a gloomy person. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, or not trying hard enough to look on the bright side. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I had a watercolor class, my quilting circle, and a lovely book club that I always looked forward to so much. But lately... well, I feel terrible admitting this, because I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy. But lately, I'll get everything ready, and then... But some days... I just completely forget to have lunch. It just slips my mind entirely. And then other days... oh, it's terrible, I'll just stand right there at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets. And the funny thing is, nothing even tastes right to me anymore. \n\nI know I shouldn't be eating like that, I really do know better. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for not keeping up with my proper meals. Is it... is it normal for your appetite to get all mixed up like that when you're feeling a bit down? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I wouldn't say I'm exactly restless, no. I'm usually quite good at sitting quietly. I try very hard to stop when I notice I'm doing it. \n\nDo you think... is it alright that I'm experiencing both of those things? The slowing down and the pacing? I can manage perfectly well on my own most days. I certainly wouldn't want anyone to think I was becoming a nuisance or... or dragging them down. \n\nI suppose... well, sometimes I might worry just a tiny bit that they have their own busy lives and I shouldn't take up too much of their time.
